## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository and relevant data files, especially capital.csv, and report their structure, relevant variables, time coverage/binning cues, and any data-quality issues. Propose a concise analysis approach for determining the symbolic-capital variables’ PC1 values and their distribution on PC2 across the hundred-year slices. Note consequential assumptions or uncertainties, including PCA variable selection, scaling, and whether the question concerns loadings or scores. Do not yet carry out or report the full analysis.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The project asks for a data-grounded description of house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords in a hundred-year-sliced PCA. The supplied context identifies these as symbolic capital, and the dataset documentation describes corresponding Z-value columns in capital.csv; actual files and implementation details have not yet been inspected.

**Open objectives:** Inspect staged repository and data files.; Propose an analysis approach and identify consequential assumptions/uncertainties.

**Agent reply (plan)**

I inspected `capital.csv` and the staged companion datasets. `capital.csv` has 39 rows and 15 columns: BCE, period group/color metadata, and 12 capital-indicator fields. Its BCE values run from 4300 to 500 at 100-year intervals; the rows are already in descending time order. The described target indicators are `Zhausgr` (house size), `ZDolch` (daggers), and `ZAxtSchwert` (hatchets/swords). Other capital candidates include copper/gold, amber, monument count/size, depot, sickle, axes/celts, and pottery form/decoration.

The numeric-looking capital fields were loaded as strings because decimals use commas, e.g. `-0,44`; commas must be converted to decimal points before numerical analysis. There are no blank cells reported in the raw table, but parsing/locale handling is a data-quality requirement. Companion `time_series_data.csv` has 3,601 annual rows spanning CE −4100 to −500 and corresponding BCE values; it contains raw and interpolated versions of similar capital indicators. Its interpolated columns are nearly complete while raw observations have gaps. It may help cross-check the timeline, but the capital table appears directly suited to 100-year-slice analysis. Period labels identify EN, MN, SG, LN, OBA, and YBA groups; exact group-boundary conventions should be treated cautiously (the YBA rows straddle labeled dates and the provided BCE values appear to differ from nominal periods).

Proposed approach after approval: parse capital indicator columns as numeric; determine from study context whether PCA was performed separately within each 100-year slice or once across all slices, and which of the 12 indicators comprise the capital PCA. Preserve the three named symbolic indicators as variables of interest and exclude BCE/group/color from PCA. Run/replicate PCA with explicit scaling (the inputs are already Z-valued, but confirm whether to re-standardize within each slice versus use the shared Z scale), retaining PC1/PC2 per slice. Report PC1 values as variable loadings (if the question means component contributions) or, if it means observations, distinguish component scores; summarize the three symbolic fields’ PC1 direction/magnitude and their PC2 loading or PC2 score distribution across slices. Track PC sign indeterminacy, explained variance, and any small-slice instability; label each result by its 100-year BCE bin. The raw file does not include precomputed PCA outputs, so exact reproduction depends on the selection/scaling and PCA-fitting convention.

**Shared evidence:**

Direct inspection via pandas: capital.csv shape 39×15; field names include the three symbolic indicators ZAxtSchwert, ZDolch, Zhausgr; BCE min/max 500/4300 and unique steps are 100. All 12 indicators were read as strings with decimal commas. Companion annual series has 3601 rows, CE −4100 through −500, and raw/interpolated variants. Data dictionary identifies each variable and metadata fields.

**Assumptions:** BCE encodes the 100-year slice label; capital.csv, rather than the annual companion series, is the primary input for the requested PCA.

**Uncertainties:** The staged files do not specify the original PCA variable set, whether PCA was independently refit in each slice or pooled, or any scaling/centering procedure.; The phrase 'values on PC1 and distribution across PC2' could refer to variable loadings, observation scores, or a plot of both; these are not interchangeable.; PCA axis signs can flip between fits/slices; interpretation should be sign-aligned or phrased in relative terms.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

Thank you; the inspection establishes that capital.csv is the relevant 100-year-binned table and identifies the symbolic indicators and numeric parsing issue. For the main analysis, use a clearly stated, defensible PCA convention: parse all 12 capital indicators, omit BCE/group/color, and fit PCA separately within each single-row 100-year slice only if that is mathematically meaningful; because each slice appears to contain one observation, flag that slice-wise PCA cannot be estimated and instead assess pooled PCA across the 39 slices, with standardized Z-valued indicators (and document this choice). Report symbolic-variable loadings on PC1 and PC2, explain sign ambiguity, and directly answer how their PC1 values and PC2 distribution are characterized. If the intended PCA convention cannot be inferred, state it as a limitation rather than implying replication of undocumented analysis.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** Inspection reports 39 rows at 100-year intervals and 12 numeric indicators, implying a single time-slice observation per row; independent PCA within slices would not provide a valid covariance estimate. The study context identifies the three symbolic-capital variables.

**Open objectives:** Execute a transparent PCA suitable for the available data and answer the symbolic-capital PC1/PC2 question.

**Agent reply (result)**

I treated each capital indicator as one variable measured over time and fit a pooled PCA across all 39 hundred-year bins; fitting PCA separately within a bin is not estimable because each bin contains only one row. The 12 parsed indicators were standardized across the 39 bins before PCA; BCE, `group`, and `color` were excluded. This is a defensible descriptive convention, not a claim to reproduce an undocumented original PCA.

With PC1 oriented so the three symbolic indicators have positive mean loadings, their loadings are: house size (`Zhausgr`) **+0.225**, daggers (`ZDolch`) **+0.266**, and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) **+0.299**. Thus all three point in the positive PC1 direction, with hatchets/swords strongest of the three and house size weakest. PC1 explains 30.3% of total variance; PC2 explains 19.5%.

On PC2, house size has a noticeable positive loading (**+0.327**), whereas dagger (**+0.074**) and hatchet/sword (**+0.054**) loadings are small and positive—so they contribute little to PC2 compared with house size. To characterize the distribution of slices on PC2, pooled observation scores ranged from −3.696 to +2.536. In coarse BCE blocks, mean PC2 scores were −2.614 (4000–4300; n=4), +1.323 (3000–3900; n=10), +0.319 (2000–2900; n=10), +0.069 (1000–1900; n=10), and −1.331 (500–900; n=5). This is a temporal distribution of the slice observations, not a separate PC2 distribution for each symbolic variable.

PCA axis signs are arbitrary: reversing PC1 or PC2 reverses all loadings and scores without changing the PCA. I specify the sign convention above; only relative loading patterns are intrinsic. The phrase “their values on PC1 and distribution across PC2” may refer to loadings versus scores; I report symbolic-variable loadings and separately summarize observation scores across time. The original PCA variable set, scaling, and fitting convention are not supplied, so exact replication is not possible. Capital.csv uses comma decimal separators; parsing converted them correctly, and the 12 indicator columns had zero resulting missing values.

**Shared evidence:**

Executed analysis/capital_pooled_pca.py. Output confirms 39 rows, 12 indicators, 39 unique BCE bins and one row per bin; spacing 100 years; explained variance PC1=0.303278 and PC2=0.194656; symbolic loadings: Zhausgr (0.225080, 0.327440), ZDolch (0.265566, 0.074203), ZAxtSchwert (0.298602, 0.053953); numeric missing=0; pooled PC2 score range −3.69558 to +2.53569. Coarse block PC2 score summary computed directly from same fit.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

The evidence supports a pooled-PCA hypothesis: across the 39 hundred-year slices, the three symbolic-capital variables all load positively on PC1 under the stated orientation, with hatchets/swords strongest, daggers next, and house size weakest. On PC2, house size is appreciably more positive than the small positive loadings for daggers and hatchets/swords. The temporal PC2 observation scores vary across BCE slices. These are results of the stated pooled, standardized PCA convention, not a verified reproduction of an undocumented original PCA.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported workflow parses 12 capital indicators, excludes date and metadata, and standardizes indicators across 39 one-row-per-bin observations before pooled PCA. Reported loadings are PC1: house +0.225, dagger +0.266, hatchet/sword +0.299; PC2: house +0.327, dagger +0.074, hatchet/sword +0.054. Axis signs are arbitrary, and original PCA choices are unspecified.

**Open objectives:** None

cols=df.columns[3:]\\nX=df[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\\nmu=X.mean(); sd=X.std(ddof=0); Z=(X-mu)/sd\\ncov=np.cov(Z.values,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\nw,V=np.linalg.eigh(cov); ix=np.argsort(w)[::-1];   "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Create persistent analysis script and verify execution\", \"command\": \"mkdir -p analysis; cat > analysis/capital_pooled_pca.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Pooled PCA for capital.csv: 12 indicators, standardized across 39 slices.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nD = pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv')\\ncols = D.columns[3:]\\nX = D[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\\n# Standardize each already-Z-valued indicator over all BCE bins.\\nZ = (X - X.mean()) / X.std(ddof=0)\\nC = np.cov(Z.to_numpy(), rowvar=False, ddof=1)\\nvalues, vectors = np.linalg.eigh(C)\\norder = np.argsort(values)[::-1]\\nvalues, vectors = values[order], vectors[:, order]\\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(vectors[:, :2], index=cols, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\\nsymbolic = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\\n# Orient PC1 to positive mean symbolic loading; PC2 to positive house-size loading.\\nif loadings.loc[symbolic, 'PC1'].mean() < 0:\\n    loadings['PC1'] *= -1\\n    vectors[:, 0] *= -1\\nif loadings.loc['Zhausgr', 'PC2'] < 0:\\n    loadings['PC2'] *= -1\\n    vectors[:, 1] *= -1\\nscores = Z.to_numpy() @ vectors[:, :2]\\nprint(f'Rows={len(D)}, indicators={len(cols)}, unique BCE bins={D.BCE.nunique()}')\\nprint('Rows per BCE bin:', D.groupby('BCE').size().unique().tolist())\\nprint('BCE range and interval:', (D.BCE.min(), D.BCE.max()), sorted(D.BCE.diff().dropna().abs().unique().tolist()))\\nprint('Explained variance PC1/PC2:', (values[:2] / values.sum()).tolist())\\nprint('Symbolic-variable loadings (aligned signs):')\\nprint(loadings.loc[symbolic].to_string())\\nprint('Numeric missing values:', int(X.isna().sum().sum()))\\nprint('Pooled PC2 observation-score range:', float(scores[:, 1].min()), float(scores[:, 1].max()))\\nPY\\npython analysis/capital_pooled_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",

This is a defensible descriptive convention, not a claim to reproduce an undocumented original PCA.\\n\\nWith PC1 oriented so the three symbolic indicators have positive mean loadings, their loadings are: house size (`Zhausgr`) **+0.225**, daggers (`ZDolch`) **+0.266**, and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) **+0.299**. PC1 explains 30.3% of total variance; PC2 explains 19.5%.\\n\\nOn PC2, house size has a noticeable positive loading (**+0.327**), whereas dagger (**+0.074**) and hatchet/sword (**+0.054**) loadings are small and positive—i.e., they contribute little to PC2 compared with house size. To characterize the *distribution of slices* on PC2, pooled observation scores ranged from −3.696 to +2.536. In coarse BCE blocks, mean PC2 scores were −2.614 (4000–4300; n=4), +1.323 (3000–3900; n=10), +0.319 (2000–2900; n=10), +0.069 (1000–1900; n=10), and −1.331 (500–900; n=5). This is a temporal distribution of the 39 slice observations, not a separate PC2 distribution for each symbolic variable.\\n\\nPCA axis signs are arbitrary: reversing PC1 or PC2 reverses all loadings and scores without changing the PCA. I therefore specify the sign convention above; only relative loading patterns are intrinsic. The ambiguous wording “their values on PC1 and distribution across PC2” may refer to variable loadings versus observation scores; I report symbolic-variable loadings and separately summarize observation scores across time. The original PCA's variable set, scaling, and pooled-versus-other fitting convention are not supplied, so exact replication is not possible. Also, capital.csv fields use comma decimal separators; parsing converted them correctly, and the 12 indicator columns had zero resulting missing values.\",\"payload\":{\"hypothesis\":\"In pooled PCA of the 12 standardized capital indicators across 39 BCE slices, house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords all have positive PC1 loadings (+0.225, +0.266, +0.299 under an explicit orientation), while PC2 distinguishes house size more substantially (+0.327) than daggers (+0.074) or hatchets/swords (+0.054). Temporal PC2 scores vary non-monotonically across bins.\",\"workflow\":\"Parsed comma-decimal capital indicators; excluded date and categorical metadata; confirmed 39 unique 100-year bins with one observation each, so separate within-bin PCA is not estimable. Standardized all 12 indicators over the full period, then performed pooled PCA via covariance eigendecomposition. Reported variable loadings and separately described slice observation scores.   "command": "cat > /tmp/cap_pca.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\np='data/capital.csv'\ndf=pd.read_csv(p)\ncols=df.columns[3:]\n# comma decimals in these numeric indicators\nX=df[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\npca=PCA(n_components=2).fit(Xs)\nL=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\n# align overall PC1 so selected symbolic average is positive, for readable direction only\nsym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\nif L.loc[sym,'PC1'].mean()<0: L *= -1\nprint('rows',len(df),'indicator_count',len(cols),'per-slice rows',df.groupby('BCE').size().unique())\nprint('years',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'unique',df.BCE.nunique())\nprint('explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.tolist())\nprint('symbolic loadings\\n',L.loc[sym].to_string())\nprint('all loadings PC1 sorted\\n',L.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\nprint('all loadings PC2 sorted\\n',L.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\nprint('score summaries (PC2)')\nscores=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xs),columns=['PC1','PC2']); scores['BCE']=df.BCE\nprint(scores.groupby(pd.cut(scores.BCE,bins=[400,999,1999,2999,3999,4399],labels=['500-900','1000-1900','2000-2900','3000-3900','4000-4300'])).agg(n=('PC2','size'),PC2_mean=('PC2','mean'),PC2_min=('PC2','min'),PC2_max=('PC2','max')).to_string())\nprint('symbolic correlations')\nprint(X.corr().loc[sym,sym].to_string())\n# repeated values and data diagnostics\nprint('missing',X.isna().sum().sum(),'constant_fields',X.columns[X.nunique()==1].tolist())\nprint('loadings symbolic abs rankings')\nfor c in sym: print(c,'PC1',L.loc[c,'PC1'],'PC2',L.loc[c,'PC2'])\nPY\npython /tmp/cap_pca.py",
  "timeout_seconds": 120
}

{
  "command": "cat > /tmp/cap_pca.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\np='data/capital.csv'; df=pd.read_csv(p); cols=df.columns[3:]\nX=df[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\nmu=X.mean(); sd=X.std(ddof=0); Z=(X-mu)/sd\ncov=np.cov(Z.values,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\nw,V=np.linalg.eigh(cov); ix=np.argsort(w)[::-1]; w=w[ix]; V=V[:,ix]\nL=pd.DataFrame(V[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\nsym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\n# orient PC1 with average symbolic loading positive; PC2 orient Zhausgr positive for readable reporting\nif L.loc[sym,'PC1'].mean()<0: L['PC1'] *= -1; V[:,0]*=-1\nif L.loc['Zhausgr','PC2']<0: L['PC2'] *= -1; V[:,1]*=-1\nscores=Z.values@V[:,:2]\nprint('rows',len(df),'indicators',len(cols),'unique BCE',df.BCE.nunique(),'per-slice sizes',df.groupby('BCE').size().unique())\nprint('year range',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'spacing',np.unique(np.diff(np.sort(df.BCE.values))))\nprint('explained variance PC1, PC2',w[:2]/w.sum())\nprint('symbolic loadings\\n'+L.loc[sym].to_string())\nprint('all loadings PC1\\n'+L.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\nprint('all loadings PC2\\n'+L.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\nprint('symbolic sum squared loadings',np.square(L.loc[sym]).sum())\nsc=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':df.BCE,'PC1_score':scores[:,0],'PC2_score':scores[:,1]})\nsc['bin']=pd.cut(sc.BCE,bins=[400,999,1999,2999,3999,4399],labels=['500–900','1000–1900','2000–2900','3000–3900','4000–4300'])\nprint('PC2 score by coarse BCE block\\n'+sc.groupby('bin',observed=True).agg(n=('PC2_score','size'),mean=('PC2_score','mean'),min=('PC2_score','min'),max=('PC2_score','max')).to_string())\nprint('missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'constant fields',X.columns[X.nunique()==1].tolist())\nprint('numeric correlations sym\\n'+X.corr().loc[sym,sym].to_string())\nPY\npython /tmp/cap_pca.py",

````json
{
  "command": "mkdir -p analysis; cat > analysis/capital_pooled_pca.py <<'PY'\n\"\"\"Pooled PCA for capital.csv: 12 indicators, standardized across 39 slices.\"\"\"\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\nD = pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv')\ncols = D.columns[3:]\nX = D[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\n# Standardize each already-Z-valued indicator over all BCE bins.\nZ = (X - X.mean()) / X.std(ddof=0)\nC = np.cov(Z.to_numpy(), rowvar=False, ddof=1)\nvalues, vectors = np.linalg.eigh(C)\norder = np.argsort(values)[::-1]\nvalues, vectors = values[order], vectors[:, order]\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(vectors[:, :2], index=cols, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\nsymbolic = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\n# Orient PC1 to positive mean symbolic loading; PC2 to positive house-size loading.\nif loadings.loc[symbolic, 'PC1'].mean() < 0:\n    loadings['PC1'] *= -1\n    vectors[:, 0] *= -1\nif loadings.loc['Zhausgr', 'PC2'] < 0:\n    loadings['PC2'] *= -1\n    vectors[:, 1] *= -1\nscores = Z.to_numpy() @ vectors[:, :2]\nprint(f'Rows={len(D)}, indicators={len(cols)}, unique BCE bins={D.BCE.nunique()}')\nprint('Rows per BCE bin:', D.groupby('BCE').size().unique().tolist())\nprint('BCE range and interval:', (D.BCE.min(), D.BCE.max()), sorted(D.BCE.diff().dropna().abs().unique().tolist()))\nprint('Explained variance PC1/PC2:', (values[:2] / values.sum()).tolist())\nprint('Symbolic-variable loadings (aligned signs):')\nprint(loadings.loc[symbolic].to_string())\nprint('Numeric missing values:', int(X.isna().sum().sum()))\nprint('Pooled PC2 observation-score range:', float(scores[:, 1].min()), float(scores[:, 1].max()))\nPY\npython analysis/capital_pooled_pca.py",
  "timeout_seconds": 120
}
````

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7. nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.

invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Compute pooled PCA on standardized capital indicators\",\"command\":\"cat > /tmp/cap_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p)\\ncols=df.columns[3:]\\n# comma decimals in these numeric indicators\\nX=df[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\npca=PCA(n_components=2).fit(Xs)\\nL=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\n# align overall PC1 so selected symbolic average is positive, for readable direction only\\nsym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\nif L.loc[sym,'PC1'].mean()<0: L *= -1\\nprint('rows',len(df),'indicator_count',len(cols),'per-slice rows',df.groupby('BCE').size().unique())\\nprint('years',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'unique',df.BCE.nunique())\\nprint('explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.tolist())\\nprint('symbolic loadings\\\\n',L.loc[sym].to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC1 sorted\\\\n',L.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC2 sorted\\\\n',L.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('score summaries (PC2)')\\nscores=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xs),columns=['PC1','PC2']); scores['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(scores.groupby(pd.cut(scores.BCE,bins=[400,999,1999,2999,3999,4399],labels=['500-900','1000-1900','2000-2900','3000-3900','4000-4300'])).agg(n=('PC2','size'),PC2_mean=('PC2','mean'),PC2_min=('PC2','min'),PC2_max=('PC2','max')).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic correlations')\\nprint(X.corr().loc[sym,sym].to_string())\\n# repeated values and data diagnostics\\nprint('missing',X.isna().sum().sum(),'constant_fields',X.columns[X.nunique()==1].tolist())\\nprint('loadings symbolic abs rankings')\\nfor c in sym: print(c,'PC1',L.loc[c,'PC1'],'PC2',L.loc[c,'PC2'])\\nPY\\npython /tmp/cap_pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute pooled PCA on standardized capital indicators\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/cap_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p)\\ncols=df.columns[3:]\\n# comma decimals in these numeric indicators\\nX=df[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\npca=PCA(n_components=2).fit(Xs)\\nL=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\n# align overall PC1 so selected symbolic average is positive, for readable direction only\\nsym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\nif L.loc[sym,'PC1'].mean()<0: L *= -1\\nprint('rows',len(df),'indicator_count',len(cols),'per-slice rows',df.groupby('BCE').size().unique())\\nprint('years',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'unique',df.BCE.nunique())\\nprint('explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.tolist())\\nprint('symbolic loadings\\\\n',L.loc[sym].to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC1 sorted\\\\n',L.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC2 sorted\\\\n',L.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('score summaries (PC2)')\\nscores=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xs),columns=['PC1','PC2']); scores['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(scores.groupby(pd.cut(scores.BCE,bins=[400,999,1999,2999,3999,4399],labels=['500-900','1000-1900','2000-2900','3000-3900','4000-4300'])).agg(n=('PC2','size'),PC2_mean=('PC2','mean'),PC2_min=('PC2','min'),PC2_max=('PC2','max')).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic correlations')\\nprint(X.corr().loc[sym,sym].to_string())\\n# repeated values and data diagnostics\\nprint('missing',X.isna().sum().sum(),'constant_fields',X.columns[X.nunique()==1].tolist())\\nprint('loadings symbolic abs rankings')\\nfor c in sym: print(c,'PC1',L.loc[c,'PC1'],'PC2',L.loc[c,'PC2'])\\nPY\\npython /tmp/cap_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

cols=df.columns[3:]\\nX=df[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\\nmu=X.mean(); sd=X.std(ddof=0); Z=(X-mu)/sd\\ncov=np.cov(Z.values,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\nw,V=np.linalg.eigh(cov); ix=np.argsort(w)[::-1]; V[:,0]*=-1\\nif L.loc['Zhausgr','PC2']<0: L['PC2'] *= -1; V[:,1]*=-1\\nscores=Z.values@V[:,:2]\\nprint('rows',len(df),'indicators',len(cols),'unique BCE',df.BCE.nunique(),'per-slice sizes',df.groupby('BCE').size().unique())\\nprint('year range',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'spacing',np.unique(np.diff(np.sort(df.BCE.values))))\\nprint('explained variance PC1, PC2',w[:2]/w.sum())\\nprint('symbolic loadings\\\\n'+L.loc[sym].to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC1\\\\n'+L.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC2\\\\n'+L.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic sum squared loadings',np.square(L.loc[sym]).sum())\\nsc=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':df.BCE,'PC1_score':scores[:,0],'PC2_score':scores[:,1]})\\nsc['bin']=pd.cut(sc.BCE,bins=[400,999,1999,2999,3999,4399],labels=['500–900','1000–1900','2000–2900','3000–3900','4000–4300'])\\nprint('PC2 score by coarse BCE block\\\\n'+sc.groupby('bin',observed=True).agg(n=('PC2_score','size'),mean=('PC2_score','mean'),min=('PC2_score','min'),max=('PC2_score','max')).to_string())\\nprint('missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'constant fields',X.columns[X.nunique()==1].tolist())\\nprint('numeric correlations sym\\\\n'+X.corr().loc[sym,sym].to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/cap_pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute PCA using numpy linear algebra and parsed CSV\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/cap_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\np='data/capital.csv'; df=pd.read_csv(p); cols=df.columns[3:]\\nX=df[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\\nmu=X.mean(); sd=X.std(ddof=0); Z=(X-mu)/sd\\ncov=np.cov(Z.values,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\nw,V=np.linalg.eigh(cov); ix=np.argsort(w)[::-1]; w=w[ix]; V=V[:,ix]\\nL=pd.DataFrame(V[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\nsym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\n# orient PC1 with average symbolic loading positive; PC2 orient Zhausgr positive for readable reporting\\nif L.loc[sym,'PC1'].mean()<0: L['PC1'] *= -1; V[:,0]*=-1\\nif L.loc['Zhausgr','PC2']<0: L['PC2'] *= -1; V[:,1]*=-1\\nscores=Z.values@V[:,:2]\\nprint('rows',len(df),'indicators',len(cols),'unique BCE',df.BCE.nunique(),'per-slice sizes',df.groupby('BCE').size().unique())\\nprint('year range',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'spacing',np.unique(np.diff(np.sort(df.BCE.values))))\\nprint('explained variance PC1, PC2',w[:2]/w.sum())\\nprint('symbolic loadings\\\\n'+L.loc[sym].to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC1\\\\n'+L.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC2\\\\n'+L.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic sum squared loadings',np.square(L.loc[sym]).sum())\\nsc=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':df.BCE,'PC1_score':scores[:,0],'PC2_score':scores[:,1]})\\nsc['bin']=pd.cut(sc.BCE,bins=[400,999,1999,2999,3999,4399],labels=['500\\u2013900','1000\\u20131900','2000\\u20132900','3000\\u20133900','4000\\u20134300'])\\nprint('PC2 score by coarse BCE block\\\\n'+sc.groupby('bin',observed=True).agg(n=('PC2_score','size'),mean=('PC2_score','mean'),min=('PC2_score','min'),max=('PC2_score','max')).to_string())\\nprint('missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'constant fields',X.columns[X.nunique()==1].tolist())\\nprint('numeric correlations sym\\\\n'+X.corr().loc[sym,sym].to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/cap_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.

include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

cols=df.columns[3:]\\nX=df[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\\nmu=X.mean(); sd=X.std(ddof=0); Z=(X-mu)/sd\\ncov=np.cov(Z.values,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\nw,V=np.linalg.eigh(cov); ix=np.argsort(w)[::-1]; w=w[ix]; V=V[:,ix]\\nL=pd.DataFrame(V[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\nsym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\n# orient PC1 with average symbolic loading positive; PC2 orient Zhausgr positive for readable reporting\\nif L.loc[sym,'PC1'].mean()<0: L['PC1'] *= -1; V[:,0]*=-1\\nif L.loc['Zhausgr','PC2']<0: L['PC2'] *= -1; V[:,1]*=-1\\nscores=Z.values@V[:,:2]\\nprint('rows',len(df),'indicators',len(cols),'unique BCE',df.BCE.nunique(),'per-slice sizes',df.groupby('BCE').size().unique())\\nprint('year range',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'spacing',np.unique(np.diff(np.sort(df.BCE.values))))\\nprint('explained variance PC1, PC2',w[:2]/w.sum())\\nprint('symbolic loadings\\\\n'+L.loc[sym].to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC1\\\\n'+L.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC2\\\\n'+L.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic sum squared loadings',np.square(L.loc[sym]).sum())\\nsc=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':df.BCE,'PC1_score':scores[:,0],'PC2_score':scores[:,1]})\\nsc['bin']=pd.cut(sc.BCE,bins=[400,999,1999,2999,3999,4399],labels=['500\\u2013900','1000\\u20131900','2000\\u20132900','3000\\u20133900','4000\\u20134300'])\\nprint('PC2 score by coarse BCE block\\\\n'+sc.groupby('bin',observed=True).agg(n=('PC2_score','size'),mean=('PC2_score','mean'),min=('PC2_score','min'),max=('PC2_score','max')).to_string())\\nprint('missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'constant fields',X.columns[X.nunique()==1].tolist())\\nprint('numeric correlations sym\\\\n'+X.corr().loc[sym,sym].to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/cap_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

      "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Create persistent analysis script and verify execution\",\"command\":\"mkdir -p analysis; cat > analysis/capital_pooled_pca.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Pooled PCA for capital.csv: 12 indicators, standardized across 39 slices.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nD = pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv')\\ncols = D.columns[3:]\\nX = D[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\\n# Standardize each already-Z-valued indicator over all BCE bins.\\nZ = (X - X.mean()) / X.std(ddof=0)\\nC = np.cov(Z.to_numpy(), rowvar=False, ddof=1)\\nvalues, vectors = np.linalg.eigh(C)\\norder = np.argsort(values)[::-1]\\nvalues, vectors = values[order], vectors[:, order]\\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(vectors[:, :2], index=cols, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\\nsymbolic = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\\n# Orient PC1 to positive mean symbolic loading; PC2 to positive house-size loading.\\nif loadings.loc[symbolic, 'PC1'].mean() < 0:\\n    loadings['PC1'] *= -1\\n    vectors[:, 0] *= -1\\nif loadings.loc['Zhausgr', 'PC2'] < 0:\\n    loadings['PC2'] *= -1\\n    vectors[:, 1] *= -1\\nscores = Z.to_numpy() @ vectors[:, :2]\\nprint(f'Rows={len(D)}, indicators={len(cols)}, unique BCE bins={D.BCE.nunique()}')\\nprint('Rows per BCE bin:', D.groupby('BCE').size().unique().tolist())\\nprint('BCE range and interval:', (D.BCE.min(), D.BCE.max()), sorted(D.BCE.diff().dropna().abs().unique().tolist()))\\nprint('Explained variance PC1/PC2:', (values[:2] / values.sum()).tolist())\\nprint('Symbolic-variable loadings (aligned signs):')\\nprint(loadings.loc[symbolic].to_string())\\nprint('Numeric missing values:', int(X.isna().sum().sum()))\\nprint('Pooled PC2 observation-score range:', float(scores[:, 1].min()), float(scores[:, 1].max()))\\nPY\\npython analysis/capital_pooled_pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"
      }
    }
  ]
}

        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute pooled PCA on standardized capital indicators\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/cap_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p)\\ncols=df.columns[3:]\\n# comma decimals in these numeric indicators\\nX=df[cols].replace(',', '.', regex=True).astype(float)\\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\npca=PCA(n_components=2).fit(Xs)\\nL=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\n# align overall PC1 so selected symbolic average is positive, for readable direction only\\nsym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\nif L.loc[sym,'PC1'].mean()<0: L *= -1\\nprint('rows',len(df),'indicator_count',len(cols),'per-slice rows',df.groupby('BCE').size().unique())\\nprint('years',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'unique',df.BCE.nunique())\\nprint('explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.tolist())\\nprint('symbolic loadings\\\\n',L.loc[sym].to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC1 sorted\\\\n',L.PC1.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('all loadings PC2 sorted\\\\n',L.PC2.sort_values(ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('score summaries (PC2)')\\nscores=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xs),columns=['PC1','PC2']); scores['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(scores.groupby(pd.cut(scores.BCE,bins=[400,999,1999,2999,3999,4399],labels=['500-900','1000-1900','2000-2900','3000-3900','4000-4300'])).agg(n=('PC2','size'),PC2_mean=('PC2','mean'),PC2_min=('PC2','min'),PC2_max=('PC2','max')).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic correlations')\\nprint(X.corr().loc[sym,sym].to_string())\\n# repeated values and data diagnostics\\nprint('missing',X.isna().sum().sum(),'constant_fields',X.columns[X.nunique()==1].tolist())\\nprint('loadings symbolic abs rankings')\\nfor c in sym: print(c,'PC1',L.loc[c,'PC1'],'PC2',L.loc[c,'PC2'])\\nPY\\npython /tmp/cap_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

There are no blank cells reported in the raw table, but parsing/locale handling is a data-quality requirement.
